みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企

### Page 2

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ
図表3 対内直接投資 図表4 国内供給力強化投資

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

### Page 4

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD 
Publishing 
OECD (2026), OECD Economic Surveys: Japan 2026, OECD Publishing

1 コンテンツ産業のような日本企業に競争力がある分野では、外国資本の取り込みによる事業拡大も期待される一方、独自技術
や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

5

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

 2026年10月1日から1年間（暫定）実施期間

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大
12
ASEAN5：輸出品目の中国製品との競合度（最終財） ASEAN5：ダンピング・越境EC・少額貨物への主な対応
（出所）各国公表資料、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注）2025年の中国から各国への輸出と、各国の世界向け輸出の品目構成の類似度を最
終財（資本財＋消費財）で算出。各品目について両者の輸出総額に占める構成比の小

さい方を採用し、表中はその業種別寄与度（Pt）。値が大きいほど品目構成の重なりが
大きく、中国製品との競合度が高いことを示し、市場シェアや価格競争力を直接示すも
のではない。赤のハイライトは15Pt以上、薄い赤のハイライトは5Pt以上15Pt未満。
（出所）Global Trade Atlas、RIETIより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵
― 台湾ではAI関連財の生産拡大が成長を押し上げ。韓国では交易条件の改善が所得増に寄与
 相違の背景に産業構造の違い
― 水平分業が確立した台湾電子産業が生産面で広く恩恵が波及
― メモリ主体・垂直統合モデルを特徴とする韓国半導体産業は、メモリ需給ひっ迫による価格高騰が経済を押し上げ

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ
― 価格転嫁の影響も拡大しており、原油高・円安等のコスト上昇を背景に家事用消耗品、教養娯楽用品等が加速
― 食品ではコメ価格の下落が続いた一方、コメを除いた食品価格は前月から伸び加速。
― 帝国データバンクの10月調査では、年間の食品値上げ品目数が2万品目台に到達。2025年並みの多さに

Document 044: 2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026 年 10 月 LPR 报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小 
 王青  冯琳 
2026 年 10 月 20 日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR 报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5 年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

10 月两个期限品种的 LPR 报价保持不变，符合市场预期。主要是 9 月末央行调整完善若
干货币政策工具，主要政策利率保持稳定，这意味着 10 月 LPR 报价的定价基础没有变化。另
外，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附近，报价行也缺乏主动下调 LPR 报价加点的动力。 
我们认为，近期 LPR 报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP 增速回升到 4.5%，以高技术
制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展。在此背景下，总量型货币政策保持定力，货币政
策加大逆周期调节力度优先选择结构性货币政策工具发力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR 报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9 月 29 日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000 亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000 亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000 亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼
市措施出台后，接下来将进入一段政策效果观察期。短期内总量型降息政策跟进的可能性下
降，LPR 报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
再往后看，综合考虑经济金融形势，预计 2027 年央行有望全面降息 10 到 20 个基点
（0.1 到0.2 个百分点），届时将引导 LPR 报价跟进下调。 
10 月 19 日央行宣布，“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，
公开市场隔夜回购利率 将相应成为主要政策利率。”这意味着政策利率“换锚”的大方向已
经确定，未来 LPR 报价的定价基础将由现在的央行 7 天期逆回购利率切换为隔夜逆回购利
率。背后是隔夜政策利率与货币市场隔夜利率（ DR001)之间天然匹配，不存在期限利差，更
有利于提高货币政策传导效率。我们判断，切换时点可能已为时不远，不排除较快落地的可

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
能，届时央行将公开宣布隔夜逆回购操作利率。不过，切换过程带动 LPR 报价下调的可能性
不大；未来 LPR 报价变化，还会主要跟进政策利率水平本身调整。 
10 月 19 日央行还宣布，“推动贷款利率定价基准改革，从比较单一地参考贷款市场报
价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价基准转型。”前期一些省份的银行已经开始发放以 DR
为定价基础的贷款。这意味着贷款利率市场化改革进一步推进，即从原来的单一 LPR 定价基
础，转为“LPR+DR”双锚。LPR 属于机构报价型利率，稳定性较高，而 DR 属于成交型利率，
市场化特征突出。信贷定价转为“ LPR+DR”双锚，意味着后期部分贷款利率市场化定价程度
会更高。这一方面有利于提升货币政策传导效率，加速政策利率向贷款利率传导，另一方面
也能更好满足不同市场主体的融资需求 ——对一些短期流动资金需求大、财务能力强的大型
企业来说，DR 基准贷款更能适配其信贷需求。我们判断，未来 10 年期国债收益率也可能成
为部分贷款的定价基础，背后是一些大型企业有条件在信贷市场和债券市场之间选择融资。
这意味着贷款定价基础多元化还将进一步推进。

市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。
近三月美國零售銷售月率變化(%)

顧 • AI安全監管升溫，短期壓抑市場情緒：Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度，並引入獨立第三方
評估等安全機制，引發市場對AI資本支出降溫的疑慮。不過，前沿模型開發速度放緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。
Alphabet、微軟及亞馬遜近期AI相關積壓訂單持續攀升，合計達約1.64兆美元，其中微軟及Alphabet分別突破6,000億及
5,000億美元，搭配雲端業務持續成長，顯示企業AI需求仍具韌性，相關訂單有望逐步轉化為未來數年的營收與獲利。
• 槓桿壓力逐步消化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值：美國雲端巨頭擴大AI資本支出，壓抑短期自由現金流；相較之下，亞洲
科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
AI三巨頭齊喊放慢模型研發
美國雲端巨頭與亞太科技企業自由現金流
資料來源：摩根投信，2026.8.31

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

個股報告

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程

維持買進，目標價 140 元 
 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟

元。(15x2027EPS)

股價相對大盤走勢

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

19.43% ，優於預期。  
3Q26 優於市場預期 ：市場先前預期旺矽 3Q26 營收 QoQ+0~5% ，
實際營收 QoQ+12.76% ，優於預期。  
旺矽有佈局 CPO 測試設備 ：旺矽的 Insertion 3 設備預計 4Q26
向晶圓代工 、IC 設計 、OSAT 送樣 ，1H27 有望進入量產 ；Insertion 
2 設備預計 2H26 會有較明顯的結果。  
旺矽積極擴產： 旺矽表示，目前在許多客戶端都是第一供應商，
供貨比重大多為 80% ，但之前因為產能供不應求，有外溢到第
二供應商，現在有積極擴產。  
旺矽預計 2027 年提高 PCB 的自製率： 探針卡可分為測試頭、
載板及 PCB 。旺矽表示，探針及載板均已全數自製， PCB 預計
2027 年開始量產，目標將 PCB 的自製率提升至 50% 。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 230: AI崩盘终极推演：泡沫可能在2028年初破裂，美国科技股或断崖式暴跌40%！

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI崩盘终极推演：泡沫可能在2028年初破裂，美国科技股或断崖式暴跌40%！.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

TS Lombard 认为， AI 繁荣的底层逻辑正变得极其脆弱，引爆危机⽆需投资骤降， “ 增速放
缓 ” 即可刺破泡沫。这场欢极有可能在 2028 年初⾛向终结，届时标普 500 将下跌 30% ，科技
股将断崖式暴跌 40% ，约 20 万亿美元的市值将灰⻜烟灭，美国科技股权重将⼀夜回到
ChatGPT 发布前的⽔平。美联储降息将托底，美元将受重创，未深陷 AI 的欧洲等市场将显
著跑赢美股。
摘要：
当前，全球市场正将筹码⾼度集中于⼀场史⽆前例的基础设施⼤跃进 ——AI 投资热潮。然⽽，这
场繁荣的底层逻辑正变得极其脆弱：当前的盈利主要依赖于科技巨头内部的 “ 资本⽀出循环
（ Capex recycling ） ” 和债务融资，⽽⾮外部终端⽤⼾的真实买单。
据追⻛交易台消息， TS Lombard ⾸席宏观策略师 Dario Perkins 在 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇的研报中称，根据终
极推演，这场 AI 资本⽀出狂欢极有可能在 2028 年初⾛向终结，并引发⼀场剧烈的市场重置。
报告指出，标普 500 指数将下跌 30% ，科技股将断崖式暴跌 40%，约20 万亿美元的市值将灰⻜烟

灭，美国股市的科技股权重将⼀夜回到 ChatGPT 发布前的⽔平。尽管科技巨头能凭借强劲的资
产负债表存活，但 AI ⽣态系统上游的弱势群体（如新兴云服务商、独⽴模型开发者）将⾯临违约
潮，预计将产⽣1250 亿⾄ 1900 亿美元的信贷损失。
TS Lombard 认为，宏观经济将陷⼊温和衰退： AI 资本⽀出的骤降将导致美国 GDP 下降约2 个百
分点，并造成约80 万个数据中⼼建设相关岗位流失。但由于私营部⻔资产负债表健康且美联储
有充⾜的降息空间，这不会演变成 2008 年式的系统性⾦融危机。
AI 投资热潮：史上最⼤基础设施豪赌，可持续性存疑
AI 资本⽀出已成为⼈类历史上规模最⼤的基础设施项⽬之⼀。美国科技公司每年在 AI 建设上的投
⼊约为 1 万亿美元，超级数据中⼼的累计投资规模已超越曼哈顿计划、阿波罗登⽉计划乃⾄⻢歇
尔计划。超⼤规模云服务商（ Hyperscalers ）的资本⽀出占美国 GDP 的⽐例已接近历史峰值。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

然⽽，这场繁荣的根基并不稳固。当前 AI ⽣态系统的收⼊增⻓，在很⼤程度上仍是 " 资本⽀出循
环 " （ capex recycling ）的产物—— ⼀家公司的资本⽀出，就是另⼀家公司的营收。只要超⼤
规模云服务商持续⼤规模投⼊，⽣态系统内部的收⼊就会持续增⻓，但这并不代表真正的可持
续性。
真正的可持续性标志，应是来⾃⽣态系统外部 —— 普通消费者和⾮科技企业⽤⼾ —— 的收⼊占
⽐显著提升。但截⾄报告发布时，这⼀⽐例⼏乎没有改善。 AI 的商业化变现仍是最⼤悬念：
OpenAI 和 Anthropic 约 80% 的收⼊来⾃仅 1% 的客⼾，⽽这些客⼾⼤多是依赖⻛险投资持续输⾎
的亏损 AI 初创企业。
报告称，当前的 AI 繁荣本质上是⼀场 “ 资本⽀出循环 ”—— 只要科技巨头不断向系统注资，收⼊就
会增⻓。但随着债务成为边缘融资的主要来源，这种模式极易受到资本⽀出放缓的致命打击。
六⼤崩盘触发器：任何⼀个都可能引爆连锁反应
报告梳理了六⼤潜在⻛险点，指出 AI 崩盘已处于 " 过度决定 " （ overdetermined ）状态 —— 以下六

⼤触发因素中的任何⼀个，都可能成为刺破泡沫的利刃。
触发器⼀：收⼊严重不达预期
多项研究显⽰，要使当前的资本⽀出获得合理回报， AI ⾏业到 2030 年的年收⼊需达到2.5 万亿⾄
3 万亿美元，约为当前⽔平的14 倍。这⼀增速在如此短的时间内⼏乎难以实现，尤其是在中国开
源（开放权重）模型迅速抢占市场份额的背景下。普通消费者对付费 AI 服务普遍抵触， AI 服务⾯
临商品化⻛险。
触发器⼆：信⽤⻛险急剧上升
超⼤规模云服务商此前主要依靠⾃有现⾦流投资，但债务已成为 AI 建设的边际融资来源。仅
2027 年⼀年， AI 公司预计将发⾏约7000 亿美元新债券，其中超⼤规模云服务商占4000 亿美元，
其余来⾃ AI ⽣态系统其他参与者 —— 这⼀数字超过了欧元区所有政府债券发⾏总量。

### Page 3

更令⼈担忧的是 " 表外负债 " 的快速膨胀。以 Meta 在路易斯安那州的 Hyperion 数据中⼼为例：这
是⼀个300 亿美元的投资项⽬， Meta 将其 80% 的股权出售给 Brown Owl ，成⽴名为 Beignet 的合
资公司，后者融资270 亿美元新债，由 Meta 提供担保（承诺租⽤该数据中⼼ 20 年）。由于租约被
拆分为五个四年期租约， Meta 资产负债表上只体现了极⼩部分杠杆。整个 AI ⽣态系统的隐性负
债估计已达 1.5 万亿⾄ 2 万亿美元。部分新兴云服务商（如 CoreWeave ）的融资利率已⾼达
15%。
触发器三： " 诱导利率 " 陷阱与折旧悬崖
报告将当前 AI 财务结构与 2000 年代初次贷危机中的 " 诱导利率 " （ teaser rate ）进⾏类⽐。数据中
⼼在建设期间⽆需计提折旧，但⼀旦投⼊使⽤，折旧压⼒将骤然加⼤。与此同时， AI 实验室签署
的多年期数据中⼼租约，在数据中⼼正式运营前不计⼊成本。⼀旦更多数据中⼼陆续上线， AI 企

业的财务状况可能在 12 个⽉内发⽣根本性转变。
触发器四： IPO 浪潮揭开真相
Anthropic 和 OpenAI 均计划 IPO （后者已推迟⾄ 2027 年）。历史经验表明，IPO 浪潮往往标志着周
期顶部。更重要的是， IPO 将⾸次为外界提供了解 AI ⾏业真实经济状况的窗⼝ —— ⽬前 AI 公司披
露的指标（如 " 年度经常性收⼊ "ARR ）明显经过美化，存在⼤量信息不透明问题。
触发器五：半导体周期与供应链⻛险
英伟达（ NVIDIA ）是 AI 建设的 " 镐与铲 " ，也是整个 AI 繁荣的晴⾬表。英伟达正在⼤规模开展 " 供
应商融资 " （ vendor financing ），向客⼾提供贷款以购买更多芯⽚，业内称之为 " 资产负债表即
服务 " 。根据国际清算银⾏（ BIS ）最新研究，超过 55% 流⼊ AI 企业的投资来⾃其他 AI 企业，循
环融资特征极为明显。此外，半导体需求具有⾼度周期性，存在 " ⽜鞭效应 "—— ⼀旦需求放缓，
当前的硬件短缺可能迅速转变为严重过剩。

触发器六：监管与安全⻛险
AI 技术在公众中的形象持续恶化。约 55% 的美国⼈希望放缓 AI 发展速度，仅 13% 持反对意⻅，
且这⼀结论在各主要政治群体中均成⽴。根据 2026 年 IBM/Ponemon 数据泄露成本报告，全球四

### Page 4

分之⼀的恶意企业数据泄露事件涉及某种形式的 AI 辅助攻击。⼀旦发⽣重⼤安全事故，可能引
发强烈的政治反弹和监管收紧。
" ⼆阶导数问题 " ：增速放缓就⾜以引爆危机
报告特别强调了 AI 繁荣的 " ⼆阶导数问题 " （ second derivative problem ）：AI 资本⽀出不需要绝
对下降，只需增速放缓，就⾜以引发⾦融压⼒。
这与 2000 年代初次贷危机的逻辑如出⼀辙 —— 当时触发崩盘的不是房价下跌，⽽是房价停⽌快
速上涨。在 AI 建设中，前沿实验室签署合同承诺向数据中⼼运营商⽀付数⼗亿美元，后者以此为
抵押借款建设数据中⼼。但这些实验室本⾝没有利润，其持续⽀付能⼒完全依赖于不断融资。
超⼤规模云服务商的资本⽀出就像⼀本贷款账簿：⼀旦放缓，整个繁荣就会⽡解。
市场冲击量化： 20 万亿美元财富蒸发，信⽤损失可控
⽬前科技股占美国股市市值⾼达40%，半导体板块单独占⽐接近20%。美国科技板块当前市盈
率约为30 倍（⼀年前为 40 倍），远低于互联⽹泡沫时期 70-75 倍的峰值。在 AI 崩盘情景下，报告

预测：
报告称，这些估算将科技股占美国市场的⽐重拉回⾄ ChatGPT 推出前的⽔平。
到 2027 年底，⾮超⼤规模云服务商的 AI ⽣态系统预计将有约5450 亿美元未偿债券、9200 亿美元
银⾏贷款和约6500 亿美元私⼈信贷。 AI 相关债务总规模约为2.9 万亿美元，其中约25%⾯临⻛险
敞⼝。
报告假设 2028-2030 年间累计违约率为35%，回收率为25%-50%，由此估算整体信⽤损失为
1250 亿⾄ 1900 亿美元。这⼀规模虽然可观，但对美国整体⾦融体系⽽⾔并不构成系统性威胁。
股权持有⼈将承受最⼤损失（约 20 万亿美元），但不会出现 2008 年式的信⽤损失级联和全⾯破产
潮。
最 脆 弱 的 环 节 包 括 ：新 兴 云 服 务 商 （ neoclouds ）、 甲 ⻣⽂ （ Oracle ）、 独 ⽴ 模 型 开 发 商
（ OpenAI 、 Anthropic ）以及建筑 / 设备供应商。
经济温和衰退，类似 1990 年或 2001 年
到 2028 年初，美国科技相关资本⽀出将较 2023 年上升约3 个百分点（实际 GDP 占⽐）。报告假设

完全逆转，但由于数据中⼼投资⾼度依赖进⼝，进⼝下降将部分抵消资本⽀出下滑对 GDP 的冲
击。综合各种效应，AI 数据中⼼繁荣结束将使美国 GDP ⽔平下降约 2 个百分点（ IMF 最新世界经
济展望报告得出了⼏乎相同的估算）。
报告称，整体⽽⾔，这场衰退的严重程度将与 1990 年或 2001 年相当，属于历史标准中的温和衰
退，并给出了以下三个理由：
S&P 500 整体下跌 30% ；科技股下跌 40% ；其他板块下跌约 20% ，合计蒸发市值约 20 万亿美
元。
第⼀，私⼈部⻔资产负债表健康。私⼈信贷增⻓幅度不⾜ GDP 的 10 个百分点，与 1980 年代末更
为相似，远未达到次贷危机前的⽔平，不存在系统性信⽤事件的⻛险。

### Page 5

此外，对于就业市场⽅⾯，报告指出， AI 建设热潮⽬前已创造约50 万个就业岗位，预计到 2027
年底将增⾄80 万个。若这些岗位全部消失，美国失业率将上升约50 个基点，在不考虑更⼴泛商
业信⼼影响的情况下。
第⼆，货币政策弹药充⾜。美联储⾃全球⾦融危机以来⾸次重建了充⾜的货币政策空间，有充分
的降息余地，⽆需依赖财政政策⼤规模刺激。
第三，降息将激活 "K 型经济 " 底层。⻓期受⾼通胀和紧缩货币政策压制的低收⼊群体、消费耐⽤
品需求和住宅房地产市场（存在⼤量被压抑的需求）将从降息中直接受益。历史上，互联⽹泡沫
破裂后，美联储的宽松政策迅速催⽣了住宅市场的新⼀轮泡沫，类似情景可能重演。正如互联⽹
泡沫破裂后美联储催⽣了房地产繁荣⼀样，降息将迅速刺激⾮ AI 领域的增⻓，这也是衰退将保持
温和的核⼼原因。

### Page 6

报告指出，最⼤的尾部⻛险在于：AI 崩盘可能反⽽加速企业采⽤ AI ⾃动化来削减成本。历史表
明，互联⽹相关⾃动化的⼤规模推进发⽣在 2009 年经济衰退期间，⽽⾮繁荣时期。若 AI 崩盘触
发⼤规模⾃动化浪潮，劳动⼒市场的冲击可能超出预期，导致缓慢的 " ⽆就业复苏 " 。
如何在泡沫中保全资产
报告称， AI 崩盘是典型的需求冲击，⽽⾮ 1970 年代的供给冲击（滞胀型衰退）。⻓期利率将显著
下降，债券与股票的相关性将转为负值，有利于国债的避险属性。但由于衰退程度温和，不会
回到零利率或永久量化宽松。
AI 繁荣与互联⽹泡沫的关键区别在于：这场科技热潮⾼度集中于少数美国和亚洲公司，⽽互联⽹
泡沫涉及欧洲在内的⼴泛发达市场参与。韩国作为 AI 供应链核⼼，过去 12 个⽉科技出⼝占 GDP
⽐重上升了10 ⾄ 30 个百分点，将在崩盘中遭受重创。
但欧洲等未深度参与 AI 繁荣的市场，在初期恐慌性抛售过后，将显著跑赢美国市场。这些经济体
承受了 AI 繁荣的负⾯影响（⾼通胀、⾼利率），却未能充分享受其红利， AI 繁荣结束反⽽可能带

来解脱。
报告认为，AI 崩盘对美元的冲击将远⼤于互联⽹泡沫和次贷危机。由于危机根源在于美国⾦融
体系，资⾦将流向欧元、⽇元乃⾄英镑等其他货币。全球投资者⽆需涌⼊美国国债寻求避险，
其本国债券可以发挥同等作⽤。
报告明确指出，现阶段做空美国科技股或⼤幅增加久期均不可取—— 若 AI 繁荣仍有数年可持续，
过早布局将导致严重跑输。
最优策略参照 GMO 的 Ben Inker 提出的 " 易泡沫 " （ easy bubble ）交易框架：持有多元化全球股票
组合，在繁荣延续时同样受益，在崩盘时跑赢集中押注 AI 的美国科技股。仅在 AI ⽣态系统出现明
显压⼒信号时，才逐步增配政府债券。
TS Lombard 的这份推演报告传递了⼀个清醒⽽⾮末⽇论的信号：AI 泡沫破裂不会是 2008 年，更
像是 2001 年。美国经济将经历⼀场温和衰退，⾦融体系不会崩溃，美联储有充⾜⼯具应对。

### Page 7

但对投资者⽽⾔，20 万亿美元的财富蒸发、科技股 40% 的跌幅、以及 AI 供应链国家和地区的剧
烈冲击，⾜以让任何集中押注 AI 的投资组合遭受毁灭性打击。

Document 309: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%) 
          













### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

### Page 4

5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

Exhibit 7: Earnings revisions have unusually been positive 
Consensus estimates in USD. MSCI AC World EPS

The combination of higher cost of capital and greater capital intensity in the tech sector 
has reduced the value of their future cash flows and triggered a de-rating in the biggest 
companies.  The dominant capex hyperscalers in the US now have a forward PE close to 
the average of the rest of the market.

8. Software companies have also de-rated on the back of fears of disruption from AI 
while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

Despite risk, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• Ongoing Middle East tensions are increasing volatility and raising energy, input
and shipping costs, while broadly supporting prices for agricultural products.

Impacts vary depending on idiosyncratic supply and demand conditions.
• Risks are exacerbated by the impact of a now confirmed super-sized El Niño,
which could slice off up to 1ppt of New Zealand’s GDP should on-farm
production of both milk and meat contract significantly.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg, with milk powder prices holding up better than we expected. El Niño
presents a clear upside risk to this, as it does not appear to have been reflected

in GDT auction prices to date.
• Meanwhile average New Zealand beef export prices should remain elevated over
the coming year, as lean trimming prices reverse recent declines. Sheepmeat
prices are set to ease on rising Australian supply, but remain at elevated levels.
• Kiwifruit and apple export prices are expected to remain elevated, supported
by strong demand for premium varieties, although increasing supply is likely to
keep prices largely flat rather than drive further gains.

• Export prices for New Zealand logs are set to remain broadly stable to slightly
firmer, supported by lower log supply and balanced Chinese inventories.

La Niña
El Niño
Farming is well placed to navigate impacts of a strong El Niño.
• Scientists have now confirmed that the current El Niño event will be the

strongest on record. Temperatures in the Pacific Ocean are now 3.05C above the
average, exceeding the record set in 2015.
• A supersized El Niño increases the probability of drier weather in eastern regions
of New Zealand in coming months, with wetter conditions likelier in the west and
southern areas of the country.
• The potential impact on GDP could be significant. Our previous research has
shown a severe drought could slice off as much as 1ppt from economic growth.

• Much of that is will reflect lower agriculture production, particularly in dairy,
sheep and beef farms. Increased on-farm costs and processing disruptions are
also likely.
• A 1% loss in GDP is likely an upper end estimate of the El Niño impact. A key
consideration is soil moisture levels are currently high, implying some resilience
to future drier conditions.
• Farming practices have become more resilient to prolonged dry periods.
Irrigation is more widely used than in past dry periods and snowmelt levels

appear robust, which should be of particular benefit to Canterbury farms.

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Farm input costs are on the rise reflecting two key drivers: fuel and
fertiliser costs.
• Diesel and petrol prices have increased by about 50% compared to the same
period in 2025. While average prices fell in the September quarter, they closed

the quarter at an elevated level as tensions between the US and Iran increased
and broadened to encompass Saudi Arabia and the Yemeni Houthis.
• Fertiliser prices are also significantly higher. While the fertiliser market has
stabilised after a period of extreme volatility earlier this year, prices remain
highly sensitive to developments in the Middle East.
• Nitrogen (urea) prices have fallen substantially from a mid-year spike, with an
pick up in Chinese exports now boosting supply. These prices though remain

vulnerable to supply disruptions.
• By contrast, phosphate and other sulphur-based fertiliser prices are tracking
higher and remain at elevated levels, as supply constraints, trade restrictions
and disruptions to shipping offset the impact of softening demand in some
regions of the world.

forecasts
Farmgate milk price forecast revised up on strong protein demand.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg. This is above Fonterra’s forecast midpoint of $9.50/kg (range of $8.50-

10.50), but lower than futures market pricing of just over $10/kg.
• We have also opened our forecast for the 2027/28 season at $9.50/kg. Note that
there is a wide margin of uncertainty around the forecast at this stage, and we
will review as the next season approaches.
• Dairy prices at auction have generally held up better than our expectations,
although the performance has varied across products. Trends in global demand
are shifting away from fats and towards proteins, which has boosted demand

and prices for skim milk powder in particular.
• Our forecasts do not include an assumption about the impact of El Niño, which
presents a clear upside risk to prices and a downside risk to volumes. While the
futures market is pricing a strong lift in milk powder prices over the New Zealand
summer, there has been no apparent urgency to buy at the GDT auction.
Fonterra is forecasting a 2% lift in milk volumes over the rest of this season, on
top of what was already a strong increase last season.

• Global milk production also remains ample, although it has slowed in Europe
in recent months. El Niño is less likely to affect conditions among the major
producers in the Northern Hemisphere.

Average beef export prices
US Beef (Imp 95 CL) prices, 1-month advanced
US$ c/lb US$ c/lb
Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
Export beef prices to remain elevated over the next year.
• Beef export prices remain well supported but have softened in recent
months. The weakness has been concentrated in lean beef trimming prices,

which have eased since April as high US prices attracted additional imports,
particularly from South America, while tariff-free quotas temporarily boosted
product availability.
• In contrast, premium beef prices have remained firm, supported by tight global
supplies of high-quality beef, constrained US cattle numbers, and resilient
demand in higher-end retail and foodservice channels.
• We expect that lean trimming prices will reverse recent declines in coming

months as temporary increases in supply begin to fade and underlying
shortages, including the historically small US beef herd, re-emerge.
• In 2027, lean trimming prices should remain elevated but broadly flat as
strong US demand and a small US cattle herd are largely offset by rising global
production. From 2028 onwards, expanding global beef production gradually
eases market conditions, putting pressure on prices.
• Meanwhile, favourable supply/demand fundamentals to support premium beef

prices in 2027, with expanding global production to weigh on prices from 2028.
• Overall, beef export prices are likely to remain high, although both nominal and
real prices are expected to moderate over the medium term.

TWI weighted price
Lamb export prices to remain firm, but off recent highs.
• NZ sheepmeat export prices remain near record highs, supported by
constrained global supply and resilient demand from premium markets in

Europe, the UK and North America. Export values rose strongly through 2025/26
despite only modest growth in export volumes, highlighting the strength of
underlying prices.
• Global supply remains tight due to declining sheep flocks and lower slaughter
numbers in New Zealand, the UK and parts of Europe. However, rising Australian
production and exports are expected to gradually lift global supply and increase
competition in key export markets, limiting further price gains.

• Meanwhile, stronger demand from the UK, EU and North America has improved
export returns and reduced New Zealand's reliance on China, where economic
weakness has made consumers more price sensitive and encouraged a shift
towards cheaper proteins and cuts.
• The outlook remains positive but more balanced. Tight supply should keep
prices above historical averages over the coming year, although rising Australian
exports, softer demand growth, affordability pressures, signs of buyer

resistance and (eventually) a stronger NZ dollar could see prices ease modestly
from current elevated levels into 2027.

Capacity is catching up with demand, limiting further price gains.
• NZ kiwifruit export prices have pushed to record highs, supported by strong
demand in North America, Europe, Japan and Korea, as well as continued

consumer preference for premium varieties such as SunGold.
• Export prices continue to edge higher even as a record crop and larger fruit
volumes are helping to reduce market tightness. Zespri notes increased
competition from summer fruit, pricing pressure in parts of Asia and a larger-
than-expected crop.
• Kiwifruit export prices are set to remain high but mostly flat over the next
year, as strong demand for premium varieties such as SunGold and RubyRed is

increasingly offset by rising production and price pressures in Asia.
• Apple export prices are also at record highs, supported by strong demand in
Asia, North America and India, alongside a continued shift towards higher-value
cultivars and premium varieties.
• These factors should keep export prices elevated over the next 12 months,
supporting grower revenues and profitability.
• However, further gains could be limited. Rising New Zealand production,

recovering supplies from key growing regions and larger global apple crops are
expected to increase supply availability, reducing market tightness and keeping
prices broadly stable.

Index IndexLog prices look moribund.
• China remains the dominant market for New Zealand logs, with prices highly
sensitive to construction activity, sawmill demand and daily log offtake. In that
regard, weak housing and furniture demand continue to weigh on prices.

• Chinese softwood log inventories are estimated at around 2.2 to 2.5m m³,
equivalent to roughly 40 to 45 days of inventory cover at current offtake rates.
This suggests inventory levels are broadly balanced and exerting little pressure
on log prices.
• At the same time, reduced New Zealand log supply, including lower harvesting
volumes and the wind-down of salvage logging, is providing some support to
prices. Freight costs also remain an important driver of at-wharf-gate returns,

with currently high shipping costs crimping returns to forest owners.
• NZ log export prices are expected to remain broadly stable to modestly firmer
over the next 12 months, supported by lower domestic supply, balanced Chinese
inventory levels and tentative signs of stabilisation in log demand.
• However, any recovery is likely to be gradual, with weakness in China's property
sector, uncertainty around shipping costs and softer global growth continuing to

constrain demand and cap upside potential.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
After posting a soft but better than expected first half, the
Australian economy is facing into more challenges near term
as another round of disruptions from global energy markets
combine with a further rise in interest rates and a deepening

housing downturn. Against this, business investment is in
the midst of a sustained ramp-up led by a large and growing
pipeline of data centre and energy investment projects.
Elsewhere, public demand is growing at a slower pace and
new dwelling investment is likely to moderate but both are
expected to still see positive growth. Overall, the demand
mix should see capacity pressures continue to ease but with
significant risks on either side.
Shifting political winds

Some of these risks are now in the wider policy space. The
political landscape has changed materially since the 2025
Federal Election with opinion polls showing a sustained swing
against the Labor government and a marked rise in support
for One Nation that raises the prospect of a three-horse race
in the next election, the outcome of which could be very hard
to predict (we may get a preview of this with next month’s
Victorian state election). While the next Federal election is

still a long way off – not due until well into 2028 – the shift
does appear to be having some bearing on policy. For example,
the government’s choice not to reinstate the temporary
halving in fuel excise tax that was deployed when fuel prices
first spiked back in March suggests it is less inclined towards
providing broad based cost-of-living support that might make
it harder to bring inflation back down.
Migration policy is shaping more clearly as a battleground.

A suite of changes announced by the government in mid-
September and which came into effect in October, are already
expected to slow working holidaymaker and foreign student
arrivals, moderating total net overseas migration from
around 300k current to 225k by 2028, trimming population
growth from around 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr. For its part, the main
Liberal-National opposition party has proposed to reduce net
migration to 100k in the first two years of government, before

increasing to 130k and 160k in the two years following –
which would likely see total population growth slow to around
0.7-0.8%yr initially. One Nation has proposed a period of ‘net
negative’ migration, lowering net migration by 250k a year
over the first three years of government before considering a
‘ceiling’ of 130k per year thereafter. This would see population
growth slow to around 0.2-0.3%yr initially.
While none of these policies are on the table for this year

or next, the direction of travel is clear with a risk that the
current government moves further towards curbing migrant
More challenges near term ...
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
inflows. Slower population growth basically maps one-to-
one to GDP growth although the specific flow-on impacts to
areas like housing and labour markets are more complex and
will depend on the population mix and how the demand and
supply effects balance out. For the record, we have trimmed

our own population growth forecasts in the 2028 out-year,
from 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr with a related tweak to our GDP
growth forecast for that year.
Consumer sentiment-spending gap
Near term challenges continue to centre on the consumer.
Here, the latest updates show sentiment again plumbing
historical lows but more mixed messages around spending
which is still holding up better than might be expected based
on a simple read on the consumer mood.
Certainly that mood remains deeply negative. The Westpac–

Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index dropped
4.7% to 80.4 in October, putting the month amongst the forty
worst reads since the began in the early 1970s, following
two others reads in this group back in April and June and
another eight recorded in 2022–23. Altogether this marks the
worst period of recurring extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
This has been difficult to square with the soft rather than

outright weak picture on consumer spending through most
of this year. Even when real spending was recording more
material per capita declines back in 2023-24, these were
somewhat milder than what might have been expected given
the weakness in sentiment at the time.
We explored this more fully in our August Red Book . Some
of the disconnect may be a by-product of high inflation which
‘hits different’ sentiment-wise. Cost of living pressures,

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... centred on uneven consumer, housing downturn ...
which are the central issue for consumers at the moment,
represent a broad-based but shallower squeeze rather than
the deeper shocks that typically come when big economic
downturns hit. Labour markets appear to be an important

point of distinction – despite the weakness in sentiment,
consumers have been relatively unperturbed about the jobs
outlook in recent years. Adding consumers’ unemployment
expectations to sentiment measures significantly improves its
‘fit’ as a predictor of per capita spending.
There other aspect that helps to reconcile the sentiment-
spending gap is the detailed picture across segments. There
may be one economy but there is not a single consumer.

Instead both sentiment and spending outcomes reflect the
average across a range of often quite varied conditions in
different consumer segments.
This shows through in the dispersion of sentiment across
sub-groups. The chart right shows the current distribution of
index reads across the 100-odd sub-groups we regularly track
with some selected groups called out. If the range seems wide
its because it is – measures of spread over the last two years
have been 50-60% wider than the average over the previous

three decades. Exactly why is not completely clear but the
observations point to a more fractured consumer with multiple
fault lines – including across states, metro/regional areas, voter
groups, labour force status, income levels and housing tenures.
Spending-wise, one of the clearest emerging fault lines is across
age groups. Here, there look to be three main ‘blocs’ – 18-34 year
olds (mainly millennials’); 35-54 year olds (mainly ‘gen X’); and

those aged over 55 (dominated by the ‘baby boomers’).
Demographically, the 55+ age group is both the largest and
the fastest-growing – our estimates suggest it now makes up
about 45% of all households, up 8.5pts compared to 2020. It
is also seeing significant financial shifts with the cohort coming
through carrying materially larger superannuation holdings
(reflecting a variety of factors including a longer period of
compulsory contributions that have earned compounding

returns) and more housing wealth. Again, our rough estimates
suggest average household wealth in this group is around
$2.5m up over $800k since 2020 (reflecting both the
aforementioned demographic shifts and valuation changes).
At the same time, the group has low debt levels – it is largely
untouched by interest rate increases and probably benefits
slightly insofar as this lifts interest income both directly and
indirectly via super holdings.
For 18-34 year olds, incomes are more important than balance

sheets, meaning inflation poses more of a constraint. For 35-
54 year olds, income is less of a constraint but high debt levels
poses a big challenge as rate rises increase servicing costs.
All of which is to say that it should come as little surprise that
spending by the 55+ age group has been running stronger
than the other age groups. Breakdowns from our Westpac
Consumer Panel show average non fuel spend has been rising
at a 5% annual pace over the last six months amongst those

aged 55+. That compares to a more sedate 2.7-2.9% pace 
across the other age groups, which given the current pace of 
inflation, implies small declines in real, inflation-adjusted terms.
These demographic contrasts also account for the observed 
strength in key bellwether spending categories like new 
vehicle purchases. The total number of new car purchases 
by consumers is up close to 4%yr, led by strong growth in 
EVs. Notably, industry figures suggest the purchase of used 
cars is down sharply, by close to 9%yr (used car sales are 
not well covered in the statistics and largely bypass official 
ABS measures because the are mainly transfers between 
households rather than a new addition). 
Pulling these clues together, at least some of the resilience in 
spending looks to be a form of ‘wealth effect’ where balance 
sheet considerations are more important than the real purchasing 
power of incomes. While the impact of employment, wages, 
inflation and interest rate changes will still likely be the dominant 
driver of shifts in spending growth, the nature of this wealth 
effect and how it evolves from here could be a key swing factor. 
Housing downturn deepens
And where that swing factor may start to turn is in response 
to a deepening downturn in Australia’s housing market. The 
combined impact of interest rate rises and major changes to 
housing-related tax policy announced in the Federal budget is 
continuing to weigh heavily. We have been flagging for some 
time that uncertainty could see a more significant ‘air pocket’ 
emerge with very thin trading conditions and a sharper move 
lower in prices. That risk is crystallising with turnover already 
down 20% and prices off 8%. 
With the September interest rate rise and an expected hike 
in November still to come, more weakness is likely near term. 
We revised our house price forecasts lower with a decline of 
around 8% now expected for calendar 2026, with a peak-to-
trough decline of just over 10%. 
... wealth effects an uncertain swing factor 
This constitutes a more material housing downturn. The 
peak to trough fall is set to be biggest since the beginning of 
the national price measures in 1980, although prices in real, 
inflation-adjusted terms recorded larger falls during the early-
1990s and early-1980s recessions with capital city measures 
also suggesting there were larger real price falls during the 
recessions in the mid-1970s and early 1960s. 
It also points to a bigger housing-related drag on growth. 
As discussed previously, the main channels are through 
new dwelling investment, turnover and wealth effects. New 
dwelling investment is still carrying solid momentum (the 
number of dwelling under construction up 10% over the year 
to June) but is expected to see a slightly more pronounced 
slowing. Turnover is also expected to be slightly weaker. 
However, it is the wealth effects from price declines that is the 
area to watch. Our rough estimates suggest this will account 
for most of the –0.8ppts on annual GDP growth over the 
year to June 2027. The emphasis here though is on ‘rough’. 
Wealth effects have seeming disappeared for long periods 
and their precise timing is very unclear, e.g. how should the 
drag from the current downturn be viewed against what 
were presumably strong positives coming off the much larger 
preceding gains? Some of this could be due to ‘assymmetric’ 
behaviour, with effects playing out very differently when there 
wealth is rising or falling.
On balance, we expect the impact to see consumer spending 
‘muddle through’ rather than fall back to the weaker growth 
seen during the more intense cost of living and interest 
rate shocks in 2023-24. This partly reflects the indirect 
support coming from growth more generally – the AI-related 
investment boom, which we have covered at length in previous 
reports, a key factor helping to hold GDP growth up around 
1.5%yr and prevent a more material weakening in labour 
market conditions. For the consumer though this will continue 
to feel like a struggle with growth flat in per capita terms 
and a shakier picture around housing that will play out very 
differently for some segments.

MIDDLE EAST
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should
ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could

push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating
global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite crude exports through the Strait of Hormuz

recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels in late
September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back
to normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings
have risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving
visible transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo
transfers in the Gulf of Oman have increased.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly.

Ship-to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts cause
delays and some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships
are also available, with many redeployed to other markets.
Higher insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-
China tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher
than a year ago. With prompt barrels scarce, the gap between
physical and futures prices widened again, with Dated Brent
around US$136/bbl in early October against US$101/bbl for

front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes
on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the

Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened to
an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October, from
US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the government’s excise
tax cut returning to normal, this has lifted national diesel and
petrol prices to around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a

return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average
around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around

US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of this
reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi attacks
on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below its
